    <script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    // Queues hbspt.forms.create() calls made before HubSpot's async-loaded
    // v2.js has finished loading, then replays them once it's ready
    // (v2.js's own onload="hubcache.letsgo()" call). Lets the forms.net
    // script load with `async` without breaking forms whose inline
    // hbspt.forms.create({...}) call would otherwise run before hbspt exists.
    window.hubcache = window.hubcache || {
      forms: [],
      letsgo: function () {
        while (hubcache.forms.length) {
          hbspt.forms.create(hubcache.forms.shift());
        }
      }
    };
    if (typeof window.hbspt === 'undefined') {
      window.hbspt = {
        forms: {
          create: function (c) { hubcache.forms.push(c); }
        }
      };
    }
  </script>

<form class="search-form" role="search" method="get" id="" action="https://www.group-ib.com/">
  <input type="search" placeholder="Search" name="s" id="search-input" value=""/>

<style>
  .hidden {
    display: none;
  }
</style>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
  const openSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.open-search-popup')];
  const closeSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.close-search-popup')];
  const searchPopup = document.querySelector('.search-popup');

  const closePopup = () => {
    searchPopup.style.display = 'none';
    openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.add('hidden'));
  };

  openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(openSearchPopupBtn => {
    openSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', (e) => {
      e.preventDefault();

      searchPopup.style.display = 'block';
      openSearchPopupBtn.classList.add('hidden');
      closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    });
  });

  closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(closeSearchPopupBtn => {
    closeSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', () => {
      closePopup();
    });
  });

  document.addEventListener('click', (event) => {
    if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target) && !openSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target)) && !closeSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target))) {
      setTimeout(() => {
        if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target)) {
          closePopup();
        }
      }, 100);
    }
  });
</script>    </div>

                            <h1 class="header__title title-h2 mb-24 mt-md-48 mt-lg-64 mt-xl-64">Switching side jobs</h1><p class="header__text mb-xs-48 mb-64 mb-lg-96 mb-xl-96">Links between ATMZOW JS-sniffer and Hancitor</p><div class="post-info__date mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><div class="body2">August 17, 2022 · <span id="reading-time"></span> min to read · Threat Intelligence</div></div><div class="post-info__image mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><picture><source srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-2-min.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img data-no-lazy="" fetchpriority="high" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-2-min.jpg" alt="" class="webpexpress-processed"></picture></div><div class="post-info__tags"><div class="post-info__tag body2">ATMZOW</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">JS-sniffer</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">Threat Intelligence</div></div>                        </div>
                    </div>
                </div>
                <div class="blog-post-content" id="blog-post-content">
                    <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>The hacker group ATMZOW and its JavaScript-sniffer became known in 2020, thanks to the Malwarebytes researchers, when the group <a href="https://twitter.com/MBThreatIntel/status/1215693928764063744" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">installed</a> a JS sniffer on a website that was collecting donations for victims of the Australia bushfires.</p>
<p>However, based on a specific obfuscation technique used by the group, we can <a href="https://blog.sucuri.net/2015/10/massive-magento-guruincsite-infection.html" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">track its activities back</a> to 2015 as &#8220;Magento Guruincsite malware&#8221;. Moreover, one of the first domain names, used by the group, was created in 2016.</p>
<p>According to Group-IB Threat Intelligence data, <strong>ATMZOW has successfully infected at least 483 websites belonging to the domain zones of Italy, Germany, France, UK, Australia, India, Brazil etc</strong>. since the beginning of 2019.</p>
<p><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-23405" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201200%20675'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="top level domains of websites infected by ATMZOW" width="1200" height="675" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-1.webp" /><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-23405" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-1.webp" alt="top level domains of websites infected by ATMZOW" width="1200" height="675" /></noscript></p>
<p><strong>Group-IB specialists collected information about ATMZOW’s recent activity and found ties with a phishing campaign targeting clients of a US bank</strong> based on the same JS obfuscation technique and a connection between the domain names used for the JS sniffer and the phishing domains on account of the same email address used. Further analysis showed that <strong>the same phishing kit was used during the activity of Prometheus TDS</strong>, when an unknown adversary used phishing pages as a final redirect when distributing Hancitor malware. <strong>With moderate confidence, we can conclude that both the ATMZOW JS sniffer campaign and related phishing attacks could have been conducted by the Hancitor group.</strong></p>
<h2>ATMZOW: recent activity</h2>
<p>In May 2022 <strong>Group-IB specialists discovered that ATMZOW started using Google Tag Manager</strong> (GTM) <strong>to deliver malicious payloads</strong>. Google Tag Manager is a tag management system that allows website owners to quickly and easily update various code snippets known as tags on websites and mobile apps.</p>
<p>The hackers created a Google Tag Manager link with ID GTM-WNV8QFR and started using legitimate GTM code to inject JS sniffers. Injection starts with a common GTM snippet.</p>
<div id="attachment_23406" style="width: 946px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-2.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23406" decoding="async" class="wp-image-23406 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20936%20408'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Google Tag Manager snippet with the ATMZOW attacker's ID" width="936" height="408" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-2.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23406" decoding="async" class="wp-image-23406 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-2.png" alt="Google Tag Manager snippet with the ATMZOW attacker's ID" width="936" height="408"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-23406" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 1: Google Tag Manager snippet with the attacker&#8217;s ID</p></div>
<p>This GTM script contains a specific tag (&#8220;<em>vtp_html</em>&#8220;) with the next stage injector.</p>
<div id="attachment_23407" style="width: 946px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23407" decoding="async" class="wp-image-23407 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20936%20304'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Google Tag Manager script with the ATMZOW attacker's injector" width="936" height="304" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23407" decoding="async" class="wp-image-23407 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min.png" alt="Google Tag Manager script with the ATMZOW attacker's injector" width="936" height="304"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-23407" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 2: Google Tag Manager script with the attacker&#8217;s injector</p></div>
<p>Executing the script loaded by Google Tag Manager appends the injector to the DOM of the infected website.</p>
<div id="attachment_23408" style="width: 946px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-2.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23408" decoding="async" class="wp-image-23408 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20936%20162'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="The ATMZOW attacker’s injector" width="936" height="162" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-2.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23408" decoding="async" class="wp-image-23408 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-2.png" alt="The ATMZOW attacker’s injector" width="936" height="162"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-23408" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 3: The attacker’s injector</p></div>
<p>The injector checks if the current user&#8217;s address in the address bar contains a &#8220;<em>checkout</em>&#8221; substring. If it does, the injector loads the final payload from <em>https://designestylelab[.]com/css/</em>. The script loaded from <em>https://designestylelab[.]com/css/</em> is a sample of the ATMZOW JS sniffer, but it contains an additional layer of obfuscation.</p>
<div id="attachment_23409" style="width: 946px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23409" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23409 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20936%20628'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="ATMZOW sample with additional obfuscation" width="936" height="628" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23409" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23409 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min.png" alt="ATMZOW sample with additional obfuscation" width="936" height="628"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-23409" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 4: ATMZOW sample with additional obfuscation</p></div>
<p>If we remove the junk symbols from the long string in this sample, we obtain a Base64-encoded string. After decoding, we obtain an ATMZOW sample with its common obfuscation.</p>
<div id="attachment_23410" style="width: 946px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23410" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23410" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20936%20494'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="ATMZOW sample" width="936" height="494" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-1.webp" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23410" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23410" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-1.webp" alt="ATMZOW sample" width="936" height="494" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-23410" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 5: ATMZOW sample</p></div>
<p>After decrypting the strings used in this sample, we obtain a clean script of the ATMZOW JS sniffer.</p>
<div id="attachment_23411" style="width: 946px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-min-1.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23411" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23411 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20936%20766'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="sample of the ATMZOW JS sniffer" width="936" height="766" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-min-1.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23411" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23411 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/7-min-1.png" alt="sample of the ATMZOW JS sniffer" width="936" height="766"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-23411" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 6: Use of a fake payment form in a sample of the ATMZOW JS sniffer</p></div>
<div id="attachment_23412" style="width: 946px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-min-1.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23412" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23412 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20936%20560'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Exfiltration address in a sample of the ATMZOW JS sniffer" width="936" height="560" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-min-1.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23412" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23412 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/8-min-1.png" alt="Exfiltration address in a sample of the ATMZOW JS sniffer" width="936" height="560"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-23412" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 7: Exfiltration address https://gvenlayer[.]com/track/ in a sample of the ATMZOW JS sniffer</p></div>
<h2>Phishing campaign</h2>
<p><strong>In January 2022</strong> <strong>Group-IB specialists detected several phishing pages targeting clients of a US-based bank</strong>. The pages used IDN domain names. A noteworthy fact about the pages is that they have a JavaScript script, which was presumably obfuscated<strong> by the same tool as used by ATMZOW for the group’s samples of JS sniffers.</strong></p>
<p><div id="attachment_23413" style="width: 1440px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23413" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23413" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201430%20592'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Screenshot of a JS script" width="1430" height="592" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/9-min-1.webp" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23413" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23413" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/9-min-1.webp" alt="Screenshot of a JS script" width="1430" height="592" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-23413" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 8: Screenshot of a JS script from the page https://xn--keyvigatrs-key-7oc4531jsva[.]com/ktt/cmd/logon</p></div>Since then we have detected only 7 unique domains used for phishing pages with a similar obfuscated JS:</p>
<ul>
<li>xn--kys-nvigatorky-zp8g5mna[.]com</li>
<li>xn--kynavigatos-ky-pwc6541jna[.]com</li>
<li>navlgator-kcy[.]com</li>
<li>xn--kyavigator-ky-jjc7914ima[.]com</li>
<li>xn--ky-vigatorkey-kjc9383i4ka[.]com</li>
<li>xn--key-vigatrs-key-wuc9688j1wa[.]com</li>
<li>xn--keyvigatrs-key-7oc4531jsva[.]com</li>
</ul>
<h2>Connection between the JS-sniffer and the phishing campaign</h2>
<p>When we detected the same obfuscation technique on a phishing website for the first time, we hypothesized that the method was not unique to ATMZOW, but that other hackers could be using the same obfuscator. However, further analysis of the group&#8217;s recent activity showed additional evidence that<strong> attacks involving the JS sniffer and the phishing campaign were conducted by the same group.</strong></p>
<p>When <strong>ATMZOW</strong> started using Google Tag Manager as the initial stage of their infections, they used a website with the domain name <em>designestylelab[.]com</em> as the storage location for their payloads. With a patented technology named Group-IB Graph, we discovered that this domain was created using the email address <em><a href="/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection" class="__cf_email__" data-cfemail="f1909f9f94c49d989f9585b186989f9e9282df929e9c">[email&#160;protected]</a></em>. The same email address was used to create two more IDN domains for phishing pages targeting clients of the same bank as the pages with the ATMZOW-like obfuscation, which we first detected in January 2022:</p>
<ul>
<li>kẹy-ņạvigatorkey.com (xn--ky-vigatorkey-kjc9383i4ka[.]com)</li>
<li>key-ņạvigatọrskey.com (xn--key-vigatrskey-8oc4531jsva[.]com)</li>
</ul>
<div id="attachment_23414" style="width: 946px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-1.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23414" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23414 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20936%20848'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="connection between JS sniffer storage and phishing domains" width="936" height="848" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-1.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-23414" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-23414 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/10-min-1.png" alt="connection between JS sniffer storage and phishing domains" width="936" height="848"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-23414" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 9: Graph shows a connection between JS sniffer storage and phishing domains</p></div>
<p>In addition, one of these domains created with the email address <em><a href="/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection" class="__cf_email__" data-cfemail="3a5b54545f0f5653545e4e7a4d535455594914595557">[email&#160;protected]</a></em> (<em>xn--ky-vigatorkey-kjc9383i4ka[.]com</em>) <strong>was tagged as a phishing page with ATMZOW-like obfuscated JS script</strong>. It was detected on January 27, 2022.</p>
<p>Based on the same JS obfuscation technique and the connection between the domain names used for the JS sniffer and the phishing domains (the same email address), we can conclude with a high degree of reliability that <strong>both campaigns were conducted by the same threat group.</strong></p>
<h2>Connection between the phishing campaign and Hancitor malware</h2>
<p>While analyzing <a href="https://www.group-ib.com/blog/prometheus-tds/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Prometheus TDS</a>, <strong>Group-IB Threat Intelligence specialists detected several cases when phishing pages targeting clients of the same bank were used as a final redirect after downloading the malicious payload distributed by Prometheus TDS</strong>. In all cases, the malicious payload was Microsoft Office documents with a macro that dropped Hancitor malware.</p>
<p>For example, a common method of distribution via Prometheus TDS was the use of Google Docs with a link to the compromised website with <a href="https://urlscan.io/result/53833a56-df34-4777-a0d1-385ef7050bf6/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener"><em>Prometheus.Backdoor</em> installed</a>. In this case, the <em>Prometheus.Backdoor</em> link was <em>hXXp://www.swingsidebilbao[.]com/wp-content/plugins/contact-form-7/includes/block-editor/carl.php</em>. If a user clicked on the link, they would receive a malicious Office document &#8220;<em>0210_4367220121562.doc</em>&#8221; (SHA1: <em>be3effcb9069ac6d66256c8246fde33e55980403</em>) and then would be <a href="https://app.any.run/tasks/b2e2d82b-de03-4300-bf96-1aef1944926e/" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">redirected to the phishing website</a><em> hXXps://xn--keynvigatorkey-yp8g[.]com/ktt/cmd/logon0210_4367220121562.do</em>c. If the user opened the malicious document and enabled macros then, the document would drop the Hancitor DLL (<em>SHA1: 17693bca881ec9bc9851fcb022a664704c048b9d</em>).</p>
<p>As we can see, in this case the hackers used IDN domains again to spoof a real banking website. Moreover, if we compare unique URLs generated while analyzing phishing pages from both campaigns, it is clear that <strong>both phishing pages were created using the same kit, with slight modifications.</strong></p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="table"><div class="table__wrapper"><table width="100%" data-table-width="50%;50%">
<thead>
<tr>
<th width="50%">Unique URLs of phishing page with ATMZOW obfuscation</th>
<th width="50%">Unique URLs from Hancitor-related phishing page</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td width="50%">https://xn--keyvigatrs-key-7oc4531jsva[.]com/ktt/cmd/logon/</td>
<td width="50%">https://xn--keynvigatorkey-yp8g[.]com/ktt/cmd/logon0210_4367220121562.doc</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="50%">https://xn--keyvigatrs-key-7oc4531jsva[.]com/ktt/cmd/logon</td>
<td width="50%">https://xn--keynvigatorkey-yp8g[.]com/ktt/cmd/logon</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="50%">https://xn--keyvigatrs-key-7oc4531jsva[.]com/a3dcb4d229de6fde0db5686dee47145d_wait.gif</td>
<td width="50%">https://xn--keynvigatorkey-yp8g[.]com/wait.gif</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="50%">https://xn--keyvigatrs-key-7oc4531jsva[.]com/a3dcb4d229de6fde0db5686dee47145d_security.php</td>
<td width="50%">https://xn--keynvigatorkey-yp8g[.]com/security.php</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>Based on the information we collected, we can therefore conclude with a high degree of reliability that <strong>both clusters of phishing pages are part of a long-running phishing campaign conducted by one cybercriminal group.</strong></p>
<h2>IoCs</h2>
<h3>Phishing websites with ATMZOW-like obfuscation</h3>
<ul>
<li>xn--kys-nvigatorky-zp8g5mna.com</li>
<li>xn--kynavigatos-ky-pwc6541jna.com</li>
<li>navlgator-kcy.com</li>
<li>xn--kyavigator-ky-jjc7914ima.com</li>
<li>xn--ky-vigatorkey-kjc9383i4ka.com</li>
<li>xn--key-vigatrs-key-wuc9688j1wa.com</li>
<li>xn--keyvigatrs-key-7oc4531jsva.com</li>
</ul>
<h3>Phishing websites detected in the Hancitor campaign with Prometheus TDS</h3>
<ul>
<li>xn--avigatorkey-56b.com</li>
<li>xn--nvigators-key-if2g.com</li>
<li>xn--keynvigatorkey-yp8g.com</li>
<li>xn--xprss53-s8ad.com</li>
</ul>
<h3>ATMZOW GTM ID</h3>
<ul>
<li>GTM-WNV8QFR</li>
</ul>
<h3>ATMZOW JS sniffer storage</h3>
<ul>
<li>designestylelab.com</li>
</ul>
<h3>ATMZOW JS sniffer gates</h3>
<ul>
<li>gvenlayer.com</li>
<li>metahtmlhead.com</li>
<li>winsiott.com</li>
<li>congolo.pro</li>
<li>vamberlo.com</li>
<li>nmdatast.com</li>
<li>seclib.org</li>
</ul>
</div>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    const postcontent = document.getElementById('blog-post-content');
    const h2counter = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2');

    for (i = 0; i < h2counter.length; i++) {
        const h2elementtag = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2')[i];
        const h2element = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2')[i].innerHTML;
        let postnavitem = document.createElement('a');
        postnavitem.classList.add('post-navigation__item', 'subtitle2', 'scrollto');
        postnavitem.innerHTML = h2element;
        const postnavlink = postnavitem.innerHTML;
        let str = postnavlink;
        str = str.replace(/<[^>]*>/g, '');
        str = str.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9\s\u0400-\u04FF]/g, '');
        str = str.replace(/\s+/g, '-').toLowerCase();
        str = str.replace(/^-+|-+$/g, '');
        postnavitem.classList += ' ' + str;
        postnavitem.href = '#' + str;
        h2elementtag.id = str;
        document.getElementById('post-navigation__wrapper').appendChild(postnavitem);
    }

    const sections = document.querySelectorAll('h2');
    const navItems = document.querySelectorAll('.post-navigation__item');

    window.addEventListener('scroll', () => {
        let current = '';
        sections.forEach((section) => {
            const sectionTop = section.offsetTop;
            const sectionHeight = section.clientHeight;
            if (pageYOffset >= sectionTop - sectionHeight / 10) {
                current = section.getAttribute('id');
            }
        });

        navItems.forEach((a) => {
            a.classList.remove('active');
            if (a.classList.contains(current)) {
                a.classList.add('active');
            }
        });
    });

    // SMOOTH SCROLL
    var $root = jQuery('html, body');


    jQuery('a[href^="#"].scrollto').click(function() {
        var href = jQuery.attr(this, 'href');
        var headerHeight = 100;

        $root.animate({
                scrollTop: jQuery(href).offset().top - headerHeight
            },
            300,
        );

        return false;
    });

    // SMOOTH SCROLL WITH NO HASH
    var $root = jQuery('html, body');


    jQuery('a[href^="#"].scrolltonohash').click(function() {
        var href = jQuery.attr(this, 'href');
        var headerHeight = 100;

        $root.animate({
                scrollTop: jQuery(href).offset().top - headerHeight
            },
            300
        );

        return false;
    });
</script>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    function readingTime() {
        const text = document.getElementById("blog-post-content").innerText;
        const wpm = 225;
        const words = text.trim().split(/\s+/).length;
        const time = Math.ceil(words / wpm);
        document.getElementById("reading-time").innerText = time;
    }
    readingTime();
</script>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    document.getElementById("share-url").value = window.location.href;
    document.getElementById("share-url-mobile").value = window.location.href;
    jQuery('.share-url__copy').click(function() {
        jQuery('.share-url__copy').addClass('success');
        navigator.clipboard.writeText(window.location.href);
        setTimeout(() => {
            jQuery('.share-url__copy').removeClass('success');
        }, 1500);
    });
</script>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    var carousel123 = new Swiper('.carousel123', {
        grabCursor: true,
        pagination: {
            el: '.swiper-pagination',
            clickable: true,
        },
    });

    var authorsMoreThan2Swiper = new Swiper('.authors-more-than-2-swiper', {
        grabCursor: true,
        pagination: {
            el: '.swiper-pagination',
            clickable: true,
        },
        breakpoints: {
            0: {
                slidesPerView: 'auto',
                spaceBetween: 16,
                slidesPerView: 1,
                slidesPerGroup: 1,
            },
            620: {
                spaceBetween: 24,
                slidesPerView: 2,
                slidesPerGroup: 2,
            },
        },
    });

    function positioningSidebar() {
        let sidebar = document.getElementById('sidebar');
        let sidebarHeight = sidebar.offsetHeight;
        let windowHeight = document.documentElement.clientHeight;
        if (windowHeight < (sidebarHeight + 48)) {
            let sidebarAuthors = sidebar.querySelector('.sidebar__authors');
            let sidebarMorePosts = sidebar.querySelector('.sidebar__more-posts');
            let authorsHeight = sidebarAuthors ? sidebarAuthors.offsetHeight : 0;
            let morePostsHeight = sidebarMorePosts ? sidebarMorePosts.offsetHeight : 0;
            let sidebarTop = authorsHeight + morePostsHeight + 140;
            jQuery(sidebar).css('top', -sidebarTop + 'px');
        } else {
            jQuery(sidebar).css('top', '72px');
        }
    }
    window.addEventListener('resize', positioningSidebar);
    positioningSidebar();

    if (localStorage.getItem('theme')) {
        if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'dark') {
            jQuery('body').addClass('theme--dark');
        } else if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'light') {
            jQuery('body').addClass('theme--white');
        }
    } else {
        localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark');
        jQuery('body').addClass('theme--dark');
    }

    jQuery('.theme-toggle').click(function() {
        if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'dark') {
            localStorage.setItem('theme', 'light');
        } else {
            localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark');
        }
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('no-transition');
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('theme--dark');
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('theme--white');
        setTimeout(() => {
            jQuery('body').toggleClass('no-transition');
        }, 250);
    });
    jQuery('.share-toggle').click(function() {
        jQuery('.share-toggle').toggleClass('active');
        jQuery('.share-widget').toggleClass('show');
    });
</script>
